# Art requests

The ledger of every picture the app is waiting for. Mikey makes each one in Leonardo.ai (Nano Banana 2), exports PNG or JPG, and uploads it with its serial; Claude resizes it to about 1200 px, converts it to WebP, and drops it at the path the serial maps to. A missing picture shows a placeholder with its serial in the app until then.

Serials: **S** story scene, **C** core character reference sheet, **I** item pictures for matching modules, **G** games and coloring pages, **L** logo. Numbers never change once given.

Where a file goes: `S7` becomes `art/stories/S7.webp`. Character sheets (`C`) are references for Leonardo only and are not shipped in the app.

Prompt rule (Mikey's template, 2026-09-23): `[Core Subject Concept], [Playful Modifier]. Style: Clean 3D vector illustration, vibrant and engaging color palette, minimalist [scene] background, balanced lighting. Professional educational graphic style, clear focal point, uncluttered layout, [Visual Anchor].` The modifier and the anchor come from the subject: Science is wonder-filled and vibrant with a dynamic close-up; History is friendly and whimsical yet respectful with a heroic composition and soft cinematic lighting; Math is cheerful and neat with distinct geometric shapes; Reading and Writing are imaginative and expressive, character-focused with dreamy lighting. Every negative prompt begins with text, words, letters, labels, numbers.

Where a story stars a core character, the prompt names the character sheet to use as the reference (Leonardo's character reference feature), so the same person looks the same in every scene.

## Coloring pages (L serials)

Letter coloring pages are Leonardo line art, not code. Deliver each as a square PNG or JPG named by its serial; Claude resizes it to about 1200 px, converts to WebP and saves it at art/coloring/<serial>.webp, where the page shows it the moment it exists. Until then the card wears a placeholder with the serial. Ink is blended onto the page so the black lines stay visible through a child's color.

| Serial | Page | Scene | Leonardo prompt | Negative prompt | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | letter-a | The letters A and a, side by side, to color | Coloring page line art for young children: a large capital letter A on the left and a large lowercase letter a on the right, side by side on one line, wide friendly rounded sans-serif letterforms, thick clean black outlines, hollow letters with white insides ready to be colored, plain white background, nothing else on the page. Style: crisp black and white line art, coloring book, no shading. | text other than the two letters, extra letters, numbers, shading, gray fill, color, gradients, watermark, signature, blurry, jagged lines, background scenery | Needed |

## Maps (M serials)

The Where is it? game keeps its tap regions in code as invisible hit areas over these paintings, so the painting carries no
names or borders that would give the answer away. Mikey paints every map in Leonardo for accuracy (2026-09-23); the code
regions are never drawn. The file goes to `art/maps/<serial>.webp`; until it arrives, the game shows a placeholder card with the
serial ("This map is being painted") and real students do not see the game at all. When a painting arrives, the hit areas are
traced to match it (tools/map-overlay.mjs draws them over the painting so they can be checked). Each map is a 4:3 or square view that matches the region polygons in `MAPS` (src/logic.mjs):
the continents map is the whole world in a 100 by 62 box, Texas fills a square, the United States a 100 by 64 box.

| Serial | Where | Scene | Prompt | Negative prompt | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | continents | The whole world, equirectangular view, the seven continents as soft green and tan land on a light blue sea, Antarctica along the bottom edge | A gentle world map of the seven continents on a light blue sea, land in soft greens and tans with a little relief shading, no country borders, no names, the whole world from the Arctic to Antarctica in one wide view. Style: Clean 3D vector illustration, vibrant and engaging color palette, minimalist background, balanced lighting. Professional educational graphic style, clear focal point, uncluttered layout, soft blue ocean. | text, words, letters, labels, numbers, logos, watermark, signature, blurry, extra fingers, extra limbs, deformed hands, photorealistic skin, borders, country lines, flags | Needed |

## Keeping the cast consistent in Leonardo

See docs/LEONARDO-BRIEF.md: make the C sheets first, then reference them in every scene. Ages and family ties are never written into a story's text; they live in these prompts and in docs/CHARACTERS.md.

## Coloring pages (G)

None requested yet. When Mikey wants better coloring pages: black-and-white line art, thick clean outlines, no shading, no text, white background; these go in as draw-mode pages. No placeholders show in the app for coloring pages.
